This is exact feasible coordinate descent (pairwise coordinate descent) for nonconvex f? f is max of quadratics; for fixed other pair, f is max of lines, convex in Δ. Coordinate descent for nonconvex? Each coordinate step decreases f (minimizes exact f over that 1D subspace), so f nonincreasing. It converges to coordinate-wise local minimum w.r.t. these pair directions. This is attractive and simple. No λ. It enforces sum exactly at all times. It doesn't guarantee global but heuristic. Need choose pair directions to cover enough directions. If we update each i with partner next, we span all coordinates? The last update may not have direct control over some variable independently? But over cycles, each variable changes as active and as compensator. Since directions are e_i - e_p, the span of cycle graph edges is connected, so can reach any sum-preserving direction (differences) by combinations. Pseudo:
def pair_coordinate_update(h, F, i, p, max_iter=40):
    old_i=h[i]; old_p=h[p]
    lo = max(0.0, old_i+old_p-1.0)
    hi = min(1.0, old_i+old_p)
    # current old_i should be feasible, due floating maybe clip: lo=min(lo,old_i); hi=max(hi,old_i)
    dlo = lo - old_i; dhi = hi - old_i
    if dhi - dlo <= tol: return 0
    s = get_slope(i,p,h)
    # We can first evaluate slopes at current to maybe endpoint? But golden.
    # Use Fibonacci/golden with vectorized max.
    gr = (3 - sqrt(5))/2
    a = dlo; b = dhi
    x1 = b - gr*(b-a); x2 = a + gr*(b-a)
    f1 = np.max(F + s*x1); f2 = np.max(F + s*x2)
    # But F + s*x large arrays allocate each eval. Could precompute F and use dot? okay.
    for ...:
       if f1 <= f2: b=x2; x2=x1; f2=f1; x1=b-gr*(b-a); f1=max(...)
       else: a=x1; x1=x2; f1=f2; x2=a+gr*(b-a); f2=max(...)
    optimum = (a+b)/2
    Δ = optimum
    # To ensure nonincrease, compare φ(Δ) to current f = max(F). If φopt > f due numerical, choose 0 or step? Since exact minimization should ≤.
    new_f = np.max(F + s*Δ)
    if new_f > maxF + 1e-12: maybe use smaller? But convex optimum ≤ current for any feasible including 0, so if due numerical, set Δ=0 or line search.
    update h[i]+=Δ; h[p]-=Δ; F += s*Δ; project bounds small.
    return newf - oldf.

def normalize_sum(h,target):
  h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  for _ in range(100): diff=target-h.sum(); if abs(diff)<1e-15 break; h+=diff/n; h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  # final adjust free variables evenly.
  diff=target-h.sum(); if abs(diff)>0:
    if diff>0: free=np.where(h<1-1e-12)[0]; if len: add = diff/len(free); h[free]=np.clip(h[free]+add,0,1)
    else: free=np.where(h>1e-12)[0]; ...
  # ensure one or two coordinates adjusted to remove residual while bounds.
  diff=target-h.sum()
  if abs(diff)>1e-14:
    # choose coordinate with max room in direction
    if diff>0: idx=np.argmax(1-h); add=min((1-h[idx]),diff); h[idx]=min(1,h[idx]+add); diff-=add; while diff... choose others.
    else similar.
  return clip
Works.

def eval_phi(v, F, s):
    val = F + s*(v-old)
    m = val.max()
    active = np.where(val > m - 1e-14)[0]
    return m, s[active].min(), s[active].max()

def local_multipass(h, n, lam, start_time, time_limit, order_mode=0, max_passes=None):
  target = n/2.0
  F = compute_F(h)
  best_local_h = h.copy(); best_local_val = np.max(F)
  for pass_i in range(max_passes):
    if time.time() - start_time > time_limit: break
    order = np.arange(n) or np.random.permutation(n)
    lam_i = lam
    for k in order:
      # optional early time check per coordinate if pass long
      c = get_coeff(k,h,F_offset)
      delta = coordinate_search(h[k], F, c, lam_i)
      if delta != 0:
         h[k] += delta
         F += c*delta
         # bound clean

    # project sum exact
    h = project_sum(h, target)
    F = compute_F(h)
    val = np.max(F)
    if val < best_local_val - 1e-12:
       best_local_val = val; best_local_h = h.copy()
    # adjust lam if lam_adapt? if lam is None use adapt
  return best_local_h, best_local_val

Detailed functions:

compute_c5:
def compute_c5(h):
  n=len(h)
  if n==0: return 0.0
  dx=2.0/n
  return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full'))*dx)

compute_F:
def compute_F(h):
  n=len(h); off=n-1; m=2*n-1; dx=2.0/n
  F=np.empty(m,float); comp=1.0-h
  F[off]=dx*np.dot(h, comp)
  # positive
  for d in range(1,n):
     L=n-d
     F[off+d]=dx*np.dot(h[:L], comp[d:])
     F[off-d]=dx*np.dot(h[d:], comp[:L])
  return F
This loop uses slices creating views; okay. Could precompute comp. For n up to 500, loops 500.

get_coeff:
def get_coeff(h,k,off):
  n=len(h); c=np.zeros(2*n-1,float)
  c[off]=1.0-2.0*h[k]
  left=k
  right=n-1-k
  if left>0:
    rev = h[k-1::-1]
    c[off+1:off+left+1] -= rev
    # negative
    c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] += (1.0 - rev)
  if right>0:
    fwd = h[k+1:k+right+1]
    c[off+1:off+right+1] += (1.0 - fwd)
    c[off-1:off-right-1:-1] -= fwd
  return c
Need check negative slice for left>0 and right>0 can overlap as discussed. But if we do c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] with left=2, off=4: slice 3:1:-1 => 3,2. Then if right=2, c[3:1:-1] again, adds/subtracts. Good. If left/right different, ranges overlap partially; separate additions fine.

minimize_line:
def minimize_line(F, coeff, old, lam, max_iter=45):
  # Returns delta. Uses subgradient binary.
  # First check active at old
  mF = np.max(F)  # Could pass current_max to avoid recompute? We have maxF. But okay; F length small. For speed pass cur_max.
  # We'll make signature minimize_line(F, coeff, old, lam, cur_max=None)
  if cur_max is None: cur_max = float(np.max(F))
  active_old = F >= cur_max - 1e-12
  if np.any(active_old):
     slopes = coeff[active_old] + lam
     if slopes.min() <= 1e-12 and slopes.max() >= -1e-12:
        return 0.0
  # binary
  lo=0.0; hi=1.0
  # Could restrict based on sign at old? If slopes max<0 -> optimum right of old, so lo=old? But if old near 0? We can use lo=old if max<0? Since derivative negative at old, function decreasing initially; minimum to right. Actually if derivative negative at old, for v<old derivative? Could be less? Convex derivative nondecreasing; if at old negative, minimizer >old. So set lo=old. If derivative positive, minimizer <old, set hi=old. This reduces interval.
  if slopes.max() < -1e-12:
     lo = old
  elif slopes.min() > 1e-12:
     hi = old
  else:
     # already checked 0, but if active tol missed, maybe return 0? 
     pass
  # But what if slopes interval at old contains 0 due no active? not. If old at boundary and derivative sign? okay.
  best_v = old; best_val = cur_max
  # evaluate candidate function: phi(v) = max(F + coeff*(v-old))? Since lam common.
  def phi? no closure. We'll inline.
  for _ in range(max_iter):
     if hi - lo < 1e-7: break
     mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
     vals = F + coeff*(mid-old)
     mv = float(np.max(vals))
     # update best maybe maybe mid with lam? lam common add lam*(mid-old) but common so for comparison just vals.
     if mv < best_val - 1e-15:
        best_val=mv; best_v=mid
     active = vals >= mv - 1e-12
     smin = coeff[active].min() + lam
     smax = coeff[active].max() + lam

     elif smin > 1e-12:
        hi = mid
     else:
        # mid optimal
        # maybe evaluate endpoints of active interval? Not necessary
        best_v=mid; break
  # also check endpoints 0,1 and old
  for cand in (0.0,1.0,old):
     if cand<0 or cand>1: continue
     vals = F + coeff*(cand-old)
     mv = float(np.max(vals))
     if mv < best_val - 1e-15:
        best_val=mv; best_v=cand
  # evaluate best_v maybe and clip
  if best_v < 0: best_v=0
  elif best_v >1: best_v=1
  # final: ensure nonincrease? Compute value at best_v
  vals = F + coeff*(best_v-old)
  mv = float(np.max(vals)) + lam*(best_v-old)? For comparison with old, lam common? At old old included 0. If using vals without lam, value difference excluding lam*(best-old). To compare candidates, must include λ(v-old). The minimizer of max lines + λv is different? I made a crucial mistake: Adding λ(v-old) to every F_l is common for a fixed v but varies with v; when taking max over lines, adding common doesn't change the max at each v? Actually max_l(a_l + b_l*v) + λv is not same as max_l(a_l + (b_l+λ)*v) because λv is common and can be pulled out: max_l(line_l(v)) + λv = max_l(line_l(v) + λv) = max_l(line_l(v) + λv) Wait line_l(v)=F_l+coeff_l(v-old). λ(v-old) is same for all l, so max_l(F_l+coeff_l(v-old)+λ(v-old)) = max_l(F_l+coeff_l(v-old)) + λ(v-old). Thus if we define modified slopes coeff+λ, the max is equivalent. But if we evaluate vals=F+coeff*(v-old) and later add λ(v-old), the active set for subgradient is lines maximizing vals (λ common). To compare different v, we need total φ_λ(v)=vals_max + λ(v-old). Minimizing this is equivalent to minimizing max(vals + λ(v-old)) = max((F+coeff*(v-old)) + λ(v-old)). The subgradient slopes of this combined max are (coeff+λ) for active lines of vals (since λ common). So binary active based on vals (without λ), slopes coeff+λ. Good. For comparing candidate v values, we should compute val = max(vals) + lam*(v-old), not max(vals) alone. The derivative sign uses slopes including λ. So minimize_line should use combined value for best_v.

minimize_line code with no closure:
def minimize_line(F, coeff, old, lam, cur_max, max_iter=45):
  m=cur_max
  active_old = F >= m - 1e-12
  if active_old.any():
    sl = coeff[active_old] + lam
    if sl.min() <= 1e-11 and sl.max() >= -1e-11:
      return 0.0
    if sl.max() < -1e-11:
      lo = old
    elif sl.min() > 1e-11:
      hi = old
    else: # if 0 in interval (should not) lo=old hi=old
      return 0.0
  else:
    lo=0.0; hi=1.0
  # But if old=0 and sign positive? hi=old=0 => no update. If old=1 and sign negative lo=1.
  # If sign says optimum right but old=1, no.
  best_v=old; best_total=m
  for _ in range(max_iter):
    if hi <= lo + 1e-8: break
    mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
    vals = F + coeff*(mid-old)
    mv=float(np.max(vals)); total=mv + lam*(mid-old)
    if total < best_total - 1e-14:
       best_total=total; best_v=mid
    active = vals >= mv - 1e-11
    smin=coeff[active].min()+lam; smax=coeff[active].max()+lam
    if smax < -1e-11:
       lo=mid
    elif smin > 1e-11:
       hi=mid
    else:
       best_v=mid; break
  # candidate 0,1,old
  for cand in (0.0,1.0,old):
     vals = F + coeff*(cand-old)
     mv=float(np.max(vals)); total=mv+lam*(cand-old)
     if total < best_total - 1e-14:
        best_total=total; best_v=cand
  if best_v < 0: best_v=0.0
  elif best_v >1: best_v=1.0
  return best_v-old

Potential issue: active set with vals >= mv - 1e-10 could include many inactive due flat; slopes min/max may straddle zero falsely. Use 1e-12. F values ~0.4; numerical. Could use tol=1e-10? To avoid too many, maybe 1e-12. If active too broad, early stop may happen when 0 in broad slopes even if not optimal. But broad active only for identical values; if many max equal over interval, flat optimum maybe okay. Could be too conservative? Use 1e-12.

However active_old check: If multiple F max, slopes may straddle 0 even if moving slightly could reduce? For convex function at old, if 0 in subgradient, old is minimizer. Yes.

Optimization state:
def coordinate_pass(h, F, n, lam, order, start_time, stop_time):
  off=n-1
  for k in order:
    if time.time() >= stop_time: break
    cur_max = float(np.max(F))
    old = h[k]
    coeff = get_coeff(h,k,off)
    delta = minimize_line(F, coeff, old, lam, cur_max)
    if abs(delta) > 1e-10:
       h[k] = old + delta
       # clip slight
       if h[k] < 0.0: h[k]=0.0
       elif h[k] > 1.0: h[k]=1.0
       F += coeff * (h[k]-old)
  return h,F

F update with h[k]-old (maybe clipped, not exactly delta if clip). If clipped, the searched v not used; better to set delta_effect = h[k]-old, but F linear update with c*delta_effect; okay if delta_effect small from clip. Actually if delta caused 1+1e-16 and clipped, difference tiny.

After pass: project_sum; F=compute_F(h). Note: If h values changed by projection, F recomputed.

local_search function:
def local_search(h, n, lam, start_time, stop_time, max_passes, rng=None, adapt=False, initial_passes=...):
  target=n/2.0
  h=h.copy()
  h=project_sum(h,target)
  F=compute_F(h)
  best_h=h.copy(); best_val=compute_c5(h)  # or maxF
  off=n-1
  lam_cur = float(lam)
  pass_count=0
  no_improve=0
  while pass_count < max_passes and time.time() < stop_time:
     if rng is not None and (pass_count % 2 == 1):
        order = rng.permutation(n)
     else:
        order = np.arange(n)
     coordinate_pass(h,F,n,lam_cur,order,start_time,stop_time)
     pass_count +=1
     # drift and adapt lam
     sum_after = float(np.sum(h))
     if adapt:
        drift = sum_after - target
        lam_target = 0.5 * drift  # positive if need reduce (if drift positive, lam positive)
        lam_cur = 0.6*lam_cur + 0.4*lam_target
        # clip
        if lam_cur > 2.0: lam_cur=2.0
        elif lam_cur < -2.0: lam_cur=-2.0
     h=project_sum(h,target)
     F=compute_F(h)
     val = max(float(np.max(F)), 0.0)
     # Use compute_c5 maybe expensive? F max same. We'll use val.
     if val < best_val - 1e-12:
        best_val = val; best_h=h.copy(); no_improve=0
     else:
        no_improve +=1
     # Stop if many no improve and lam fixed? Could but time.
     if no_improve > 8 and adapt is False:
        # maybe break fixed lam if converged
        break
  # final ensure
  h=project_sum(best_h,target); return h, compute_c5(h)
But if lam fixed not adapt and no improvement, break. If no_improve >30? For fixed lam with projection, if no improvement, likely local.

def local_search(h, n, lam, start_time, stop_time, max_passes, adapt_lam, rng):
    target = 0.5*n
    h = project_sum(h.astype(float).copy(), target)
    F = compute_F(h)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_val = compute_c5(h)  # maybe F max
    off = n-1
    lam_cur = float(lam)
    passes=0
    stagnation=0
    while passes < max_passes and time.time() < stop_time:
       if rng is not None:
          # Use random order sometimes: rng.random_sample() <0.5
          if rng.random_sample() < 0.33:
             order = rng.permutation(n)
          else:
             order = np.arange(n)
       else:
          order = np.arange(n)
       coordinate_pass(h, F, n, lam_cur, order, start_time, stop_time)
       passes +=1
       if adapt_lam:
          drift = float(np.sum(h)) - target
          # update lam
          lam_target = 0.7*drift
          lam_target = max(-3.0, min(3.0, lam_target))
          lam_cur = 0.7*lam_cur + 0.3*lam_target
       h = project_sum(h, target)
       F = compute_F(h)
       val = float(np.max(F))
       # Optionally if val from F slightly lower than compute_c5? Use compute_c5 for best record? For local best we can use val. But to compare to global best need objective_value. Could use compute_c5 occasionally. Since F and correlate same, okay. Use val = compute_c5(h) maybe every pass (n small). Could be more reliable. But local_search called many; compute_c5 via correlate O(n^2) but C fast. Use val = compute_c5(h) maybe okay. For n=400, correlate O(160k) each pass, 500 passes=80M C, okay. Use objective_value? evaluate_erdos maybe unknown. Use compute_c5.
       val = compute_c5(h)
       if val < best_val - 1e-12:
          best_val=val; best_h=h.copy(); stagnation=0
       else:
          stagnation +=1
       if stagnation > 25 and not adapt_lam:
          # if fixed lam and no improvement, maybe stop; if adapt, continue while lam changing? maybe 60.
          if not adapt_lam and stagnation > 12: break
          if adapt_lam and stagnation > 80: break
    best_h = project_sum(best_h, target)
    return (best_h, compute_c5(best_h))

Now, projection function exact sum. We need robust for floating target half. Implement:
def project_sum(h,target):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True)
    n = h.size
    if n == 0: return h
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    # Use iterative uniform adjustment where possible
    for _ in range(60):
        diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
        if abs(diff) <= 1e-15:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            free = h < (1.0 - 1e-15)
        else:
            free = h > (1e-15)
        if np.any(free):
            per = diff / float(np.sum(free))
            if abs(per) > 1e-15:
                h[free] += per
                h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
            else:
                break
        else:
            break
    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    # If residual, fix coordinates sequentially
    if abs(diff) > 1e-14:
        for _ in range(n):
            if abs(diff) <= 1e-14: break
            if diff > 0:
                idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
                add = min(1.0 - h[idx], diff + 1e-15)
                if add <= 0: break
                h[idx] = min(1.0, h[idx] + add)
                diff -= add
            else:
                idx = int(np.argmax(h))
                sub = min(h[idx], -diff + 1e-15)
                if sub <= 0: break
                h[idx] = max(0.0, h[idx] - sub)
                diff += sub
    # Final clip and maybe adjust one coordinate to exact within 1e-15
    h = np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-15:
       # adjust the coordinate with slack; if none, hope
       if diff > 0:
          idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h)); h[idx] = min(1.0, h[idx] + diff)
       else:
          idx = int(np.argmax(h)); h[idx] = max(0.0, h[idx] + diff)
    return np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
But adjust by diff when diff slightly over slack could break bounds; use min then sum may off. But diff residual likely tiny. Could after final, if sum off by >1e-12, choose a coordinate with more slack. Good enough.
Need ensure if target half for odd n, no coordinate at .5? The algorithm can set one to .5.

But iterative free adjustment with all free may set many values slightly above/below. If h values near 0/1, enough free. Good.

Another method bisection on shift: For target, find τ such that sum(clip(h-τ,0,1))=target. More robust. Since h projected within [0,1], we can use:
def project_sum(h,target):
  h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  # find shift τ: g(τ)=sum(clip(h-τ,0,1)) decreasing in τ. target between 0 and n.
  lo=-1.0; hi=1.0? If h in [0,1], τ from -1 to1 enough.
  for it in 60:
    mid=(lo+hi)/2

  proj=np.clip(h-mid,0,1)
  # then distribute residual on free.

def pair_pass_grid(h,F,n, order_pairs, dx, start_time, stop_time, grid_points=97, refine=True):
  off=n-1
  for i,p in order_pairs:
    if time>=stop break
    old_i=h[i]; old_p=h[p]
    lo = max(0.0, old_i+old_p-1.0); hi=min(1.0, old_i+old_p)
    if hi <= lo + 1e-12: continue
    dlo=lo-old_i; dhi=hi-old_i
    if max(abs(dlo),abs(dhi)) < 1e-10: continue
    c_i=get_coeff(h,i,off); c_p=get_coeff(h,p,off); A=c_i-c_p
    Q=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    ad=abs(i-p)
    if ad < n: # always
       if i < p:
          Q[off+ad] = -dx
          Q[off-ad] = +dx
       else:
          Q[off+ad] = +dx
          Q[off-ad] = -dx
    curmax = float(np.max(F))
    # sample
    # If interval small, maybe use binary? Use 33 points.
    G = grid_points if (dhi-dlo)>0.2 else 33
    deltas = np.linspace(dlo, dhi, G)
    # For memory, compute in loop if G*m large? Vectorize 2D:
    dmat = deltas[:,None]
    rowmax = np.max(F + A*dmat + Q*dmat*dmat, axis=1)
    idx=int(np.argmin(rowmax))
    best_delta = float(deltas[idx])
    best_val = rowmax[idx]
    # refine if idx>0 and idx<G-1
    if refine and 0<idx<G-1:
       lo_r = max(dlo, best_delta - (deltas[1]-deltas[0]) * (G/4?))

def objective_value(h):
    try:
        val = evaluate_erdos_solution(h)
    except Exception:
        val = compute_c5(h)
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        val = compute_c5(h)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        val = compute_c5(h)
    return val

`project_sum`: careful with n=0. Also target may be float. Use `np.sum` with dtype float.

Let's write project_sum robust:
def project_sum(h, target):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True)
    n = h.size
    if n == 0:
        return h
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    for _ in range(200):
        s = float(np.sum(h))
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) <= 1e-15:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            free = h < (1.0 - 1e-12)
        else:
            free = h > (1e-12)
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(free))
        if cnt == 0:
            break
        per = diff / cnt
        if abs(per) <= 1e-15:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            # If per too large due many at 1? clip handles
            h[free] += per
            np.clip(h[free], 0.0, 1.0, out=h[free])
        else:
            h[free] += per
            np.clip(h[free], 0.0, 1.0, out=h[free])
    s = float(np.sum(h))
    diff = target - s
    if abs(diff) > 1e-13:
        # sequential adjust
        for _ in range(n * 2):
            if abs(diff) <= 1e-13:
                break
            if diff > 0:
                idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
                room = 1.0 - h[idx]
                if room <= 1e-15:
                    break
                add = room if room < diff else diff
                h[idx] += add
                diff -= add
            else:
                idx = int(np.argmax(h))
                room = h[idx]
                if room <= 1e-15:
                    break
                sub = room if room < -diff else -diff
                h[idx] -= sub
                diff += sub
    # final residual exact choose coordinate
    resid = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(resid) > 1e-15:
        if resid > 0:
            # choose idx with max room
            rooms = 1.0 - h
            idx = int(np.argmax(rooms))
            h[idx] = min(1.0, h[idx] + resid)
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            h[idx] = max(0.0, h[idx] + resid)
    np.clip(h,0.0,1.0,out=h)
    return h

If final resid adds beyond room, sum may still off but resid tiny or no room. In bounded feasible target, room exists; due numeric maybe. Good.

`compute_F`: maybe use `np.dot(h[:L], comp[d:])`. For n up to 500, loop 500; okay. Could vectorize with FFT but not needed.

For left negative, add 1 - rev: `c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] += 1.0` then `-= rev`. But `+=1.0` adds to slice. Do that to reduce temp. Similarly right positive. Code:
if left>0:
  rev=h[k-1::-1]
  c[off+1:off+left+1] -= rev
  c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] += 1.0
  c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] -= rev
if right>0:
  fwd=h[k+1:k+right+1]
  c[off+1:off+right+1] += 1.0
  c[off+1:off+right+1] -= fwd
  c[off-1:off-right-1:-1] -= fwd
Need if left and right have same positive slice overlapping? c[off+1:off+left+1] and c[off+1:off+right+1] may overlap; each adds its part. Good. For negative slices, if left and right overlap, c[off-1:off-min...] +=1 and -= each. Good.
But c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] with left=0 not. For k=0 left=0 right=n-1: c[off-1:off-right-1:-1] negative lags. Good.

Potential issue: `c[off-1:off-right-1:-1]` when right=1: stop=off-2, includes off-1. When right=n-1 and off-1 to off-(n-1)-1 = -1? stop -1 exclusive? For n=5 off4 right4? but index off-1=3 down to off-right =0; stop off-right-1 = -1, slice 3:-1:-1 includes 3,2,1,0. Good.

`minimize_line`: Need avoid `coeff[active].min()` if no active. But vals max always active. Use mask. Tolerance: Use `tol = 1e-12*max(1,cur_max)`. We can pass cur_max. Implementation:
def minimize_line(F, coeff, old, lam, cur_max, max_iter=50):
    tol = 1e-12 * max(1.0, abs(cur_max))
    active = F >= cur_max - tol
    if active.any():
       slopes = coeff[active] + lam
       if slopes.min() <= 1e-10 and slopes.max() >= -1e-10:
          return 0.0
       if slopes.max() < -1e-10:
          lo = old
       elif slopes.min() > 1e-10:
          hi = old
       else:
          return 0.0
    else:
       lo=0.0; hi=1.0
    best_delta=0.0; best_total=cur_max
    # If old is not feasible? old in [0,1]
    # If lo/hi set equal old? return.
    if lo >= hi:
       # evaluate endpoints maybe
    else:
       for _ in range(max_iter):
          if hi - lo < 1e-8: break
          mid_delta = 0.5*(lo+hi)  # We used lo,hi as v? Let's switch to delta to simplify.
Actually previously lo/hi were v. Let's use delta directly:
Set search interval for delta: dlo=0-old? No old feasible. At old delta=0. If derivative negative at old, optimum delta>0; set lo=0, hi=1-old. If derivative positive, set hi=0, lo=-old. Else full [-old,1-old]. This is easier and ensures v bounds.
Let's rewrite minimize_line with delta interval:
old in [0,1]. d_min = -old, d_max = 1-old.
At old delta=0. active slopes at 0. If smax<0 (function decreasing as v increases), optimum delta>0: set lo=0, hi=d_max. If smin>0, optimum delta<0: set lo=d_min, hi=0. Else if 0 in subgrad: return 0 (still may check endpoints? but okay). If no active, full interval.
Then binary on delta interval.
Candidate v=old+mid_delta, in [0,1]. vals = F + coeff*mid_delta. active based vals. slopes = coeff[active]+lam. total = max(vals)+lam*mid_delta. If smax <0: lo=mid_delta (derivative negative -> increase delta). If smin>0: hi=mid_delta. Else optimum mid.
At end check candidates 0, d_min, d_max, mid maybe.
Return best_delta.
This avoids lo=old etc.
Pseudo:
def minimize_line(...):
  tol = 1e-11
  dmin=-old; dmax=1-old
  best_delta=0.0; best_total=cur_max
  active_at0=F>=cur_max-tol
  if active_at0.any():
    sl=coeff[active_at0]+lam
    smin=sl.min(); smax=sl.max()
    if smin <= 1e-9 and smax >= -1e-9:
       # still evaluate endpoints maybe if total lower with lam? For lam=0 old min; with lam common, 0 in subgrad of combined at old -> local/global min. return 0.
       return 0.0
    if smax < -1e-9:
       lo=0.0; hi=dmax
    elif smin > 1e-9:
       lo=dmin; hi=0.0
    else:
       return 0.0
  else:
    lo=dmin; hi=dmax
  if hi > lo + 1e-12:
    for _ in range(50):
      if hi-lo < 1e-8: break
      mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
      vals = F + coeff*mid
      mv = float(np.max(vals))
      total = mv + lam*mid
      if total < best_total - 1e-13:
         best_total=total; best_delta=mid
      active = vals >= mv - 1e-11
      slopes = coeff[active] + lam
      if slopes.max() < -1e-11:
         lo = mid
      elif slopes.min() > 1e-11:
         hi = mid
      else:
         # update best
         if total < best_total - 1e-13: best_total=total; best_delta=mid
         break
    # final candidates 0,dmin,dmax,lo,hi?
    for cand in (0.0,dmin,dmax,0.5*(lo+hi)):
       if cand < dmin - 1e-12 or cand > dmax+1e-12: continue
       vals=F+coeff*cand; mv=max; total=mv+lam*cand
       if total<best_total-1e-13: best_delta=cand
  # final evaluate best_delta
  return best_delta
Use tolerance 1e-11 for sign. Improvement threshold maybe 1e-13.
Potential: If active_at0 has smax<0 but dmax=0 (old=1), then lo=hi=0; no update. Good.

But if lam is large, 0 not in active slopes but optimum endpoint. Binary works. The total comparison includes lam; If lam constant, the minimum of combined is same as max lines with slopes coeff+lam. active set based vals correct? For subgradient of combined function φ(v)=max(F+coeff*(v-old)) + λ(v-old). active lines of max(F+...) correct. total used to choose. Good.

Now coordinate_pass:
def coordinate_pass(h,F,n,lam,order,start_time,stop_time):
  off=n-1
  for k in order:
    if time.time() >= stop_time: break
    cur_max = float(np.max(F))
    old = float(h[k])
    coeff = get_coeff(h,k,off)
    delta = minimize_line(F, coeff, old, lam, cur_max)
    if abs(delta) > 1e-10:
       newv = old + delta
       if newv < 0.0: newv=0.0
       elif newv > 1.0: newv=1.0
       eff = newv - old
       if abs(eff) > 1e-12:
          h[k] = newv
          F += coeff * eff
  return h,F

If eff != delta due clip, F update exact for eff (linear). Good.

           order = rng.permutation(n)
        else:
           order = np.arange(n)
     else:

     passes += 1

        lam_target = 0.8*drift
        if lam_target > 5.0: lam_target=5.0
        elif lam_target < -5.0: lam_target=-5.0

Pair search:
def pair_pass(h,F,n,step,dx,grid_points,start_time,stop_time):
  off=n-1; m=2*n-1
  for i in range(n):
    if time.time() >= stop_time: break
    p = (i + step) % n
    if p == i: continue
    old_i = float(h[i]); old_p = float(h[p])
    lo_v = max(0.0, old_i + old_p - 1.0)
    hi_v = min(1.0, old_i + old_p)
    if hi_v <= lo_v + 1e-12: continue
    dlo = lo_v - old_i; dhi = hi_v - old_i
    if dhi <= dlo + 1e-12: continue
    coeff_i = get_coeff(h,i,off); coeff_p = get_coeff(h,p,off); A = coeff_i - coeff_p
    Q = np.zeros(m)
    ad = abs(i-p)
    if ad < n:
       if i < p:
          Q[off + ad] = -dx
          Q[off - ad] = dx
       else:
          Q[off + ad] = dx
          Q[off - ad] = -dx
    curmax = float(np.max(F))
    # If interval tiny, maybe exact convex? use small grid.
    if dhi - dlo < 1e-5: continue
    # Choose G based on interval and n to control cost
    # Maybe G = min(321, max(65, int(32.0 / (dhi-dlo))? no.
    G = grid_points
    if G < 3: G=3
    deltas = np.linspace(dlo, dhi, G)
    # To avoid large 2D allocation for n large, use chunk? But okay.
    # vectorize: d2 = deltas[:,None]
    dcol = deltas[:, None]
    rowmax = np.max(F + A[dcol?] ... Need A broadcast. `F` shape (m,), A shape (m,), Q (m,), dcol shape (G,1). F + A*dcol + Q*dcol*dcol gives (G,m) if F broadcasts. Good.
    rowmax = np.max(F + A * dcol + Q * dcol * dcol, axis=1)
    idx = int(np.argmin(rowmax))
    best_delta = float(deltas[idx]); best_val = float(rowmax[idx])
    # check 0
    if 0.0 >= dlo - 1e-12 and 0.0 <= dhi + 1e-12:
        cur_val = curmax
        if cur_val <= best_val + 1e-12:
            best_delta = 0.0; best_val = cur_val
    # optional refinement if idx not at extremes
    if 0 < idx < G-1:
       step_d = (dhi-dlo)/(G-1)
       cl = max(dlo, best_delta - 3.0*step_d)
       ch = min(dhi, best_delta + 3.0*step_d)
       if ch - cl > 1e-9:
          deltas2 = np.linspace(cl, ch, 201)
          dcol2 = deltas2[:, None]
          rowmax2 = np.max(F + A*dcol2 + Q*dcol2*dcol2, axis=1)
          idx2 = int(np.argmin(rowmax2))
          if rowmax2[idx2] < best_val - 1e-12:
             best_delta = float(deltas2[idx2]); best_val = float(rowmax2[idx2])
    if best_val <= curmax + 1e-11 and abs(best_delta) > 1e-11:
       # apply; ensure bounds via delta interval
       h[i] = old_i + best_delta
       h[p] = old_p - best_delta
       # small cleanup: if slight out, clip and maybe adjust partner by same? Since interval safe.
       if h[i] < 0.0: h[i]=0.0
       elif h[i] > 1.0: h[i]=1.0
       if h[p] < 0.0: h[p]=0.0
       elif h[p] > 1.0: h[p]=1.0
       # If clip caused pair sum drift, set h[p] = pair_sum - h[i] where pair_sum old_i+old_p? But F update with δ_eff?
       # Better: after clip, determine eff = h[i]-old_i, but h[p] may not equal old_p-eff due both clip. We'll recompute F for consistency? Too costly per pair? Could recompute F after pair pass.
       # For now use intended delta = best_delta if within bounds. Since grid interval ensures, clip only tiny.
       F += A * best_delta + Q * (best_delta * best_delta)
       # optional fix sum for pair:
       if (h[i] < 0 or >1) or (h[p] <0 or>1):
          # recompute F? We'll after pass project and recompute. Mark? Could just project every few passes.
          pass
  return h,F
Important: If clip changes h not equal old+δ, F update with δ may be inconsistent. But clip only when δ near boundary by ~1e-16; negligible. We can avoid clipping except tiny to maintain consistency: set h[i]=old_i+best_delta, h[p]=old_p-best_delta. If due float slightly out (e.g. -1e-17), leave? It might violate bounds in evaluator. Grid `np.max(F + A*dcol + Q*dcol*dcol, axis=1)` creates 2D array. We can reduce memory by chunking over grid if n large. But likely n ≤300. Implement chunk to be safe:
def eval_grid_max(F,A,Q,deltas):
   vals_max = np.empty_like(deltas,float)
   chunk=64
   for start in range(0,len(deltas),chunk):
      d = deltas[start:start+chunk,None]
      vals = F + A*d + Q*d*d
      vals_max[start:start+chunk] = np.max(vals, axis=1)
   return vals_max

def local_search_pair(h,n,start_time,stop_time,max_passes,rng,grid_points=161):
  target=0.5*n; h=project... F=compute...
  best...
  # steps list
  steps=[]
  for s in (1,2,3,5,10,n//3,n//2,n-1,n-2,n-3): if 0<s<n and s not in steps: steps.append(s)
  if not steps: steps=[1]
  passes=0; stall=0
  dx=2.0/n
  while passes<max_passes and time<stop_time:
     # choose step: cycle, maybe random
     if rng is not None:
        if passes % 4 == 3:
           step=int(rng.randint(1,n))
        else:
           step=steps[passes % len(steps)]
     else:
        step=steps[passes % len(steps)]
     pair_pass(h,F,n,step,dx,grid_points,time.time(),stop_time)
     passes+=1
     if passes % 8 ==0:
        h=project_sum(h,target); F=compute_F(h)
     val=float(np.max(F))
     if val<best_val-1e-12: best_val=val; best_h=h.copy(); stall=0 else stall++
     if stall > 40: break
  return project, compute_c5

Let's consider if there is a way to formulate as minimizing maximum of *convex* functions? F_d = ∑ h_i(1-h_{i+d}) is not convex? Hessian zero off-diagonal? F_d = ∑ h_i - h_i h_j, Hessian off-diagonal -dx, indefinite; not convex. So max not convex.

`compute_F(h)` no dx:
def compute_F(h):
    n=len(h); off=n-1; m=2*n-1; F=np.empty(m); comp=1-h
    F[off]=dot(h,comp)
    for d in range(1,n):
       L=n-d
       F[off+d]=dot(h[:L], comp[d:])
       F[off-d]=dot(h[d:], comp[:L])
    return F
For n large, dot in C, good.

def project_sum(h,target):
  h=clip
  diff=target-sum
  if abs(diff) > 1e-6 * max(1,n): # large
     lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
     # We need sum(clip(h - tau,0,1)) decreasing with tau.
     for _ in range(80):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       s=np.sum(np.clip(h-mid,0,1))
       if s > target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
     mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
     h=np.clip(h-mid,0,1)
  # sequential residual

  else...
  final clip.

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    budget=float(budget_s)
    stop_global=t0+max(1.0,budget)-10.0
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    # Get parent
    try:
        parent = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        parent = make_default_start(96, rng)
    if parent.size ==0: parent=make_default_start(96,rng)
    parent = project_sum(parent, parent.size/2.0, use_shift=False)
    best_h=parent; best_n=parent.size; best_c5=objective_value(best_h)
    # helper inline accept:
    def? Can't closure. We'll just repeat:
    h_try, c_try; if c_try<best_c5-1e-12: update.

def resample_h(h_old,n_old,n_new,target,mode=0):
   if n_old==n_new: return project_sum(h_old.copy(),target,use_shift=True)
   x_old=2.0*np.arange(n_old)/n_old
   x_new=2.0*np.arange(n_new)/n_new
   if mode==1:
      idx=np.floor(x_new/(2.0/n_old)).astype(int) % n_old
      h_new=h_old[idx]
   else:
      h_new=np.interp(x_new, x_old, h_old, left=h_old[0], right=h_old[-1])
   return project_sum(h_new,target,use_shift=True)
Good.

def perturb_h(h,scale,rng):
  h = h + rng.randn(len(h))*scale
  return project_sum(h,len(h)/2.0,use_shift=True)

Top-level:
def random_binary_h(n,rng):
  u=rng.uniform(0,1,n)
  h=(u >= np.median(u)).astype(float)
  return project_sum(h,n/2,use_shift=True)
def thue_morse_h(n):
  vals=np.empty(n,float)
  for i in range(n):
     x=i; p=0
     while x:
       p ^= (x & 1)
       x >>=1
     vals[i]=0.0 if p==0 else 1.0
  return project_sum(vals,n/2,use_shift=True)

def grid_rowmax(F, A, Q, deltas):
    out=np.empty(deltas.shape,dtype=float)
    # Maybe if Q all zeros, avoid multiply? But Q has two nonzeros; still.
    chunk=64
    for s in range(0,len(deltas),chunk):
       d=deltas[s:s+chunk,None]
       vals=F + A*d + Q*d*d
       out[s:s+chunk]=np.max(vals,axis=1)
    return out

Top-level:
def pair_eval_max(F,A,q1,Q1,q2,Q2,deltas):
    out=np.empty(len(deltas),float)
    chunk=64
    for start in range(0,len(deltas),chunk):
       d=deltas[start:start+chunk]
       dcol=d[:,None]
       vals=F + A*dcol
       out[start:start+chunk]=np.max(vals,axis=1)
       if q1 >=0:
          v1=F[q1] + A[q1]*d + Q1*d*d
          out[start:start+chunk]=np.maximum(out[start:start+chunk], v1)
       if q2 >=0:
          v2=F[q2] + A[q2]*d + Q2*d*d
          out[start:start+chunk]=np.maximum(out[start:start+chunk], v2)
    return out
This handles Q sparse. If q1 or q2 maybe same? no. If ad? off±ad within [0,m-1]. Good.

`single_eval_max` top-level similar to pair_eval but one q. It can accept mu.
Pseudo:
def single_eval_max(F, coeff, qidx, qcoeff, mu, deltas):
   out=np.empty(len(deltas))
   chunk=64
   for s...:
      d=deltas[s:s+chunk]; dcol=d[:,None]
      vals=F + coeff*dcol
      mx=np.max(vals,axis=1)
      out[s:s+chunk]=mx
      vq=F[qidx] + coeff[qidx]*d + qcoeff*d*d
      out[s:s+chunk]=np.maximum(out[s:s+chunk], vq)
      if mu !=0: out[s:s+chunk] += mu*d
   return out
Note: If qidx is within F and coeff, vals includes q line without quadratic; vq with quadratic replaces/adds. Since q line in vals has no quadratic; maximum of full function is max(max over all linear approx (including q linear), q full). Since q full = q linear + qcoeff d². If qcoeff negative, full <= linear for δ≠0. Wait using max(max_linear, q_full) is wrong because for q, full is less than linear, but linear q value in mx may overestimate! We need exclude the linear approximation of q0 from line max, or use full for q and linear for others. If we include q linear in F+coeff*d and take max, mx may be q linear which is higher than true q_full. Then max(mx, q_full) = q_linear, overestimating objective. Need remove q idx from line max.
How to handle: In chunk, compute vals=F+coeff*dcol. Set vals[:,qidx]= -inf before max. Then add q_full. Or since only one index, can compute line max excluding q via:
  vals[:, qidx] = -1e300
  mx=np.max(vals,axis=1)
This modifies vals (already new array). Good.

For q with Q=+1, true=linear+δ² >= linear; if we don't exclude, mx may be some other line less; adding true v2 handles +1. `coordinate_pass_single`:
def coordinate_pass_single(h,F,n,mu,order,off,grid_points,start_time,stop_time):
   for k in order:
      if time>=stop break
      cur_max=float(np.max(F))
      old=float(h[k])
      coeff=get_coeff(h,k,off)
      delta, _ = choose_single_delta(F,coeff,old,mu,cur_max,n,grid_points,off)
      if abs(delta)>1e-10:
         new=old+delta
         if new<0: new=0
         elif new>1: new=1
         eff=new-old
         if abs(eff)>1e-12:
            h[k]=new
            F += coeff*eff
            F[off] -= eff*eff
  return h,F
Because q0 coeff -1 always. Wait if Q0 is -1 unscaled. Yes.

Pair_eval_max function should accept list of q indices/coeffs. We can implement generic for up to three:
def pair_eval_max(F,A,deltas,qidx,qcoeff):
  out = empty
  for chunk:
    vals=F + A*dcol
    for each qidx: vals[:,qidx] = -inf
    mx=np.max(vals,axis=1)
    out=mx
    for each q: v=F[q]+A[q]*d+Q*d*d; out=np.maximum(out,v)
  return out

Maybe implement generic:
def grid_max_quadratic(F, A, deltas, qidx, qcoef, mu=0.0):
   kq=len(qidx)
   out=np.empty(len(deltas))
   chunk=64
   NEG=-1e300
   for s...:
      d=deltas[s:s+chunk]; dcol=d[:,None]
      vals=F + A*dcol
      if kq:
         # set excluded columns
         for idx in qidx: vals[:,idx]=NEG
         mx=np.max(vals,axis=1)
      else:
         mx=np.max(vals,axis=1)
      out[s:s+chunk]=mx
      if mu !=0: out[s:s+chunk] += mu*d
      for t in range(kq):
         idx=qidx[t]
         v=F[idx]+A[idx]*d+qcoef[t]*d*d
         out[s:s+chunk]=np.maximum(out[s:s+chunk], v)
   return out

def coordinate_pass_single(h,F,n,mu,order,grid_points,refine_points,start_time,stop_time):
  off=n-1
  qidx=(off,); qcoef=(-1.0,)  # tuples recreated? okay maybe top-level constants? In function each loop? Could define outside? tuple allocation per pass not per coord.
  for k in order:
    if time.time()>=stop_time: break
    cur_max=float(np.max(F))
    old=float(h[k])
    coeff=get_coeff(h,k,off)
    delta,_=choose_grid_delta(F, coeff, -old, 1.0-old, qidx, qcoef, mu, cur_max, grid_points, refine_points)
    if abs(delta)>1e-10:
      new=old+delta; clip; eff; if eff:
         h[k]=new
         F += coeff*eff
         F[off] -= eff*eff
Return.

`project_sum` sequential+shift:
def project_sum(h,target,use_shift=False):
   h=np.array(h,dtype=float,copy=True); n=h.size
   if n==0: return h
   np.clip(h,0.0,1.0,out=h)
   if use_shift:
      lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
      for _ in range(80):
         mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
         s=float(np.sum(np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0)))
         if s > target: lo=mid
         else: hi=mid
      h=np.clip(h-0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
   for _ in range(n*2+10):
      s=float(np.sum(h)); diff=target-s
      if abs(diff)<=1e-14: break
      if diff>0:
         room=1.0-h; idx=int(np.argmax(room)); r=room[idx]
         if r<=1e-15: break
         add = r if r < diff else diff
         h[idx] += add
         if h[idx]>1.0: h[idx]=1.0
      else:
         idx=int(np.argmax(h)); r=h[idx]
         if r<=1e-15: break
         sub = r if r < -diff else -diff
         h[idx] -= sub
         if h[idx]<0.0: h[idx]=0.0
   # fallback if not exact
   s=float(np.sum(h)); diff=target-s
   if abs(diff)>1e-12:
      if diff>0:
         free=np.where(h < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
         if free.size:
            per=diff/float(free.size)
            h[free] = np.clip(h[free]+per,0.0,1.0)
      else:
         free=np.where(h > 1e-12)[0]
         if free.size:
            per=diff/float(free.size)
            h[free] = np.clip(h[free]+per,0.0,1.0)
   # final exact residual
   diff=target-float(np.sum(h))
   if abs(diff)>1e-15:
      if diff>0:
         idx=int(np.argmax(1.0-h))
         h[idx]=min(1.0,h[idx]+diff)
      else:
         idx=int(np.argmax(h))
         h[idx]=max(0.0,h[idx]+diff)
   np.clip(h,0.0,1.0,out=h)
   return h
Potential: In sequential loop, if diff > room of max, add room; diff persists, next iteration same idx now room 0, picks next. Good. For large diff, O(n) iterations, up to n*2. If target infeasible due bounds, may break. Feasible.
Use `np.argmax(h)` for diff negative; if all zero and need negative, no room. Feasible no.

def compute_F(h):

   F[off]=float(np.dot(h,comp))
   for d in range(1,n):
      L=n-d
      F[off+1+ (d-1)?] = ... Use direct.
   return F
For d loop:
for d in range(1,n):
    L=n-d
    F[off+d]=float(np.dot(h[:L], comp[d:]))
    F[off-d]=float(np.dot(h[d:], comp[:L]))

`get_coeff`:
def get_coeff(h,k,off):
   n=len(h); m=2*n-1; c=np.zeros(m,float)
   c[off]=1.0-2.0*h[k]
   left=k; right=n-1-k
   if left>0:
      rev=h[k-1::-1]
      c[off+1:off+left+1] -= rev
      c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] += 1.0
      c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] -= rev
   if right>0:
      fwd=h[k+1:k+right+1]
      c[off+1:off+right+1] += 1.0
      c[off+1:off+right+1] -= fwd
      c[off-1:off-right-1:-1] -= fwd
   return c
Wait for left positive: c[off-1:off-left-1:-1] += 1.0 adds 1 to length left. Good. If left=1, slice off-1:off-2:-1 includes off-1. Good.
For left positive and right positive, slices may overlap for same d; operations additive. Good.

`grid_max_quadratic`:
def grid_max_quadratic(F, A, deltas, qidx, qcoef, mu=0.0):
   out=np.empty(deltas.shape, dtype=float)
   NEG=-1e300
   chunk=64
   qn=len(qidx)
   for s in range(0, len(deltas), chunk):
      d=deltas[s:s+chunk]
      dcol=d[:, None]
      vals=F + A*dcol
      if qn:
         for idx in qidx:
            vals[:, idx] = NEG
         mx=np.max(vals, axis=1)
      else:
         mx=np.max(vals, axis=1)
      out[s:s+chunk] = mx
      if mu != 0.0:
         out[s:s+chunk] += mu*d
      for t in range(qn):
         idx=qidx[t]
         vc=F[idx] + A[idx]*d + qcoef[t]*d*d
         out[s:s+chunk] = np.maximum(out[s:s+chunk], vc)
   return out
Potential: qidx tuple; if duplicates, setting NEG twice. vc added twice maybe if duplicate; no. For performance, qidx list small. For single qn=1.
If d empty? not.

`choose_grid_delta`:
NEG? no.
def choose_grid_delta(F,A,dmin,dmax,qidx,qcoef,mu,cur_max,grid_points,refine_points=257):
   span = dmax - dmin
   if span <= 1e-12:
       return 0.0, cur_max
   best_delta=0.0; best_val=cur_max
   G=grid_points
   if span < 1e-3:
       G = max(7, min(G, 33))
   elif G <= 5:
       G=5
   deltas=np.linspace(dmin,dmax,G)
   vals=grid_max_quadratic(F,A,deltas,qidx,qcoef,mu)
   idx=int(np.argmin(vals))
   if vals[idx] < best_val - 1e-12:
       best_val=float(vals[idx]); best_delta=float(deltas[idx])
   # If best_delta != 0 and not at endpoints, refine
   if abs(best_delta) > 1e-12 and (0 < idx < G-1):
       step = span / float(G-1)
       lo = max(dmin, best_delta - 3.0*step)
       hi = min(dmax, best_delta + 3.0*step)
       if hi - lo > 1e-10:
           d2 = np.linspace(lo, hi, refine_points)
           vals2 = grid_max_quadratic(F,A,d2,qidx,qcoef,mu)
           idx2 = int(np.argmin(vals2))
           if vals2[idx2] < best_val - 1e-12:
               best_val=float(vals2[idx2]); best_delta=float(d2[idx2])
   # Ensure 0 candidate if not represented exactly and better
   # If best_delta==0 ok. If due grid min > curmax best_delta remains 0. If grid min lower but actual 0 maybe lower? 0 initialized so if 0 is lower than grid, best_val stays curmax.
   if best_delta < dmin: best_delta=dmin
   elif best_delta > dmax: best_delta=dmax
   return best_delta, best_val
But if grid min lower than 0, idx maybe at endpoint 0? If dmin or dmax equals 0 and best_delta at endpoint, condition internal false; okay. If best_delta endpoint and due finite G maybe not exact? includes endpoints. Good.
What if grid min lower but false because best_val initialized curmax (0) and due mu negative, 0 penalized maybe not curmax if mu *0=0 yes. Good.

`local_search_single`:

def local_search_single(h,n,mu,start_time,stop_time,max_passes,adapt_lam,rng,grid_points=None,refine_points=257):
   if grid_points is None:
      if n <= 120: grid_points=97
      elif n <= 200: grid_points=81
      else: grid_points=65
   target=0.5*n
   h=project_sum(h,target,use_shift=False)
   F=compute_F(h)
   best_h=h.copy(); best_val=compute_c5(h)
   lam=float(mu)
   passes=0; stall=0
   while passes<max_passes and time.time()<stop_time:
      if rng is not None:
         if rng.random_sample() < 0.6:
             order=rng.permutation(n)
         else:
             order=np.arange(n)
      else:
         order=range(n)
      coordinate_pass_single(h,F,n,lam,order,grid_points,refine_points,time.time(),stop_time)
      passes += 1
      if passes % 20 ==0 or (adapt_lam and passes%5==0): F=compute_F(h) maybe after projection anyway.
      drift=float(np.sum(h))-target
      if adapt_lam:
         mu_target = drift  # maybe 0.8*drift
         if mu_target > 3.0: mu_target=3.0
         elif mu_target < -3.0: mu_target=-3.0
         lam = 0.7*lam + 0.3*mu_target
      if abs(drift) > 0.05*n:
          h=project_sum(h,target,use_shift=True)
      else:
          h=project_sum(h,target,use_shift=False)
      F=compute_F(h)
      val=c5_from_F(n,F)
      if val < best_val - 1e-12:
          best_val=val; best_h=h.copy(); stall=0
      else:
          stall += 1
      if stall > (20 if adapt_lam else 10):
          break
   best_h=project_sum(best_h,target,use_shift=False)
   return (best_h, compute_c5(best_h), c5? maybe just two)
We'll return (h,c5). c5 actual.

But if adapt_lam and lam changes, no_improve 20 may break too early? Use 40. Set `if stall > (30 if not adapt_lam else 120): break`.

`local_search_pair`:
def local_search_pair(h,n,start_time,stop_time,max_passes,rng,grid_points=None,refine_points=257):
   if grid_points is None:
      if n <= 120: grid_points=97
      elif n <= 200: grid_points=81
      else: grid_points=65
   target=0.5*n; h=project_sum(h,target,use_shift=False); F=compute_F(h)
   best... val=c5_from_F
   steps=[]
   for s in (1,n-1,2,n-2,3,n-3,5,n-5,10,n-10,n//3,(n//3)-1 if >0, n//2):
       if 1<=s<n and s not in steps: steps.append(s)
   if not steps: steps=[1]
   passes=0; stall=0
   while passes<max_passes and time<stop:
      if rng is not None:
          step = int(rng.randint(1,n)) if passes%5==4 else steps[passes % len(steps)]
      else: step=steps[passes % len(steps)]
      pair_pass(h,F,n,step,grid_points,refine_points,time.time(),stop_time)
      passes+=1
      if passes % 10 ==0:
         h=project_sum(h,target,use_shift=False)
         F=compute_F(h)
      val=c5_from_F(n,F)
      if val<best_val-1e-12: best...
      else stall++
      if stall>50: break
   return project best, compute_c5

`pair_pass` top-level:
def pair_pass(h,F,n,step,grid_points,refine_points,start_time,stop_time):
   off=n-1
   for i in range(n):
      if time.time() >= stop_time: return
      p=(i+step)%n
      if p==i: continue
      old_i=float(h[i]); old_p=float(h[p])
      pair_sum=old_i+old_p
      lo = pair_sum - 1.0
      if lo < 0.0: lo=0.0
      hi = pair_sum
      if hi > 1.0: hi=1.0
      dmin=lo-old_i; dmax=hi-old_i
      if dmax <= dmin + 1e-12: continue
      coeff_i=get_coeff(h,i,off); coeff_p=get_coeff(h,p,off); A=coeff_i - coeff_p
      ad=abs(i-p)
      q1=off+ad; q2=off-ad
      if i < p:
          Q1=-1.0; Q2=1.0
      else:
          Q1=1.0; Q2=-1.0
      qidx=(off,q1,q2)
      qcoef=(-2.0,Q1,Q2)
      cur_max=float(np.max(F))
      delta,val = choose_grid_delta(F,A,dmin,dmax,qidx,qcoef,0.0,cur_max,grid_points,refine_points)
      if abs(delta) > 1e-10 and val <= cur_max + 1e-9:
          new_i=old_i+delta
          if new_i < 0.0: new_i=0.0
          elif new_i > 1.0: new_i=1.0
          new_p=pair_sum-new_i
          if new_p < 0.0: new_p=0.0
          elif new_p > 1.0: new_p=1.0
          # if new_p out after clip, adjust new_i?
          if new_p < 0.0 or new_p > 1.0:
             # fallback choose new_p clipped and new_i=pair_sum-new_p
             new_p = 1.0 if new_p > 1.0 else 0.0
             new_i = pair_sum - new_p
          eff=new_i-old_i
          if abs(eff)>1e-12:
             h[i]=new_i; h[p]=pair_sum-new_i
             F += A*eff
             d2=eff*eff
             F[off] += -2.0*d2
             F[q1] += Q1*d2
             F[q2] += Q2*d2
   return

Check q1/q2 indices: off±ad range? ad=abs(i-p) between1 and n-1. off+ad up to off+n-1=2n-2 valid. off-ad down to off-(n-1)=0 valid.
If q1/q2 equals off? ad=0 no. If pair difference and n? okay.
If i<p Q1=-1 for positive lag off+ad. We set q1=off+ad, q2=off-ad. Good. If i>p, q1=off+ad (lower as first? active upper? Derived: if i>p, q1=off+ad Q=+1, q2=off-ad Q=-1. Good.
F[off] -2.0*d2 always? If pair i,p distinct, both self terms change. Yes.
If `pair_sum` target? Pair sum not necessarily target? The sum of all h target, pair sum arbitrary. Feasible interval for new_i/new_p using pair_sum constant. Good.
If new_i after clip and pair_sum-new_i within [0,1]? Feasible interval ensures. Fallback if not.
F plus A and quadratic uses old F and eff; if new_p not exactly old_p-eff due fallback, pair sum constant means new_p=old_i+old_p-new_i=old_p-eff. Good.

`make_default_start(n, rng)`:
def make_default_start(n,rng):
    x = 2.0*np.arange(n)/n
    h = 0.5 + 0.5*np.cos(np.pi*x)
    # add small random to break symmetry
    h = h + 0.01*rng.randn(n)
    return project_sum(h, n/2.0, use_shift=True)
Good.

`thue_morse_h`: The loop bitcount. Could optimize with recurrence: tm[0]=0; for i 1..n-1: tm[i]=tm[i>>1]^(i&1) maybe. Simpler:
def thue_morse_h(n):
    vals=np.zeros(n,float)
    t=0
    for i in range(1,n):
        # parity of bitcount i
        x=i; p=0
        while x:
            p ^= (x & 1)
            x >>= 1
        vals[i]=1.0 if p else 0.0
    return project_sum(vals,n/2.0,use_shift=True)
This O(n log n). Fine. Could use recurrence for speed:
vals=np.zeros(n,float)
for i in range(1,n):
    vals[i] = vals[i >> 1] ^ (i & 1) ? But vals float 0/1; `vals[i] = vals[i >> 1] ^ (i & 1)` doesn't work with float? It will cast. Use ints:
a=np.zeros(n,int)
for i in range(1,n): a[i] = a[i>>1] ^ (i & 1)
h=a.astype(float)
Return. Good.

If `idx` maybe negative? off-ad could be -? ad≤n-1 => off-ad ≥0. off+ad ≤2n-2. Good.

Now, maybe pair q0 full value: Should be `F[off] + A[off]*d + (-2.0)*d*d`. But `A[off]` is linear derivative difference. Correct. If pair i,p same skipped. If pair difference and old values? yes.

Now, maybe direct pair quadratic coefficient sign for q1/q2 with unscaled F: For term h_low(1-h_high). If active lower i, comp upper p, q1=off+(p-i) positive, Q1=-1. If active upper, Q1=+1. Good. For q2 opposite, Q2=-Q1. So pair qcoef always -2, ±1. Good.

Let's test pair with n=2 h=[0.5,0.5], target1. F: off=1 F0=0.5; d1=0.25, d-1=0.25; max0.5. If h=[1,0], pair with i=0,p=1, δ=-? dmin=-1,dmax0; maybe no improvement. Good.

```python
import numpy as np
import time

def compute_c5(h):
    n = len(h)
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(corr) * dx)

def objective_value(h):
    try:
        val = evaluate_erdos_solution(h)
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        val = compute_c5(h)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        val = compute_c5(h)
    return val

def project_sum(h, target, use_shift=False):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True)
    n = h.size
    if n == 0:
        return h
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)

    if use_shift:
        cur = float(np.sum(h))
        if abs(cur - target) > 1e-10:
            lo = -1.0
            hi = 1.0
            for _ in range(80):
                mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
                s = float(np.sum(np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0)))
                if s > target:
                    lo = mid
                else:
                    hi = mid
            h = np.clip(h - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)

    for _ in range(2 * n + 10):
        s = float(np.sum(h))
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) <= 1e-14:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            idx = int(np.argmax(room))
            r = float(room[idx])
            if r <= 1e-15:
                break
            add = r if r < diff else diff
            h[idx] += add
            if h[idx] > 1.0:
                h[idx] = 1.0
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            r = float(h[idx])
            if r <= 1e-15:
                break
            sub = r if r < -diff else -diff
            h[idx] -= sub
            if h[idx] < 0.0:
                h[idx] = 0.0

    s = float(np.sum(h))
    diff = target - s
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        if diff > 0.0:
            free = np.where(h < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
            if free.size:
                per = diff / float(free.size)
                h[free] = np.clip(h[free] + per, 0.0, 1.0)
        else:
            free = np.where(h > 1e-12)[0]
            if free.size:
                per = diff / float(free.size)
                h[free] = np.clip(h[free] + per, 0.0, 1.0)

    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-15:
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
            h[idx] = min(1.0, h[idx] + diff)
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            h[idx] = max(0.0, h[idx] + diff)

    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h

def compute_F(h):
    n = len(h)
    off = n - 1
    m = 2 * n - 1
    F = np.empty(m, dtype=float)
    comp = 1.0 - h
    F[off] = float(np.dot(h, comp))
    for d in range(1, n):
        L = n - d
        F[off + d] = float(np.dot(h[:L], comp[d:]))
        F[off - d] = float(np.dot(h[d:], comp[:L]))
    return F

def c5_from_F(n, F):
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0
    return float(np.max(F)) * (2.0 / float(n))

def get_coeff(h, k, off):
    n = len(h)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    c = np.zeros(m, dtype=float)
    c[off] = 1.0 - 2.0 * h[k]

    left = k
    right = n - 1 - k

    if left > 0:
        rev = h[k - 1::-1]
        c[off + 1:off + left + 1] -= rev
        c[off - 1:off - left - 1:-1] += 1.0
        c[off - 1:off - left - 1:-1] -= rev

    if right > 0:
        fwd = h[k + 1:k + right + 1]
        c[off + 1:off + right + 1] += 1.0
        c[off + 1:off + right + 1] -= fwd
        c[off - 1:off - right - 1:-1] -= fwd

    return c

def grid_max_quadratic(F, A, deltas, qidx, qcoef, mu=0.0):
    out = np.empty(deltas.shape, dtype=float)
    neg_inf = -1e300
    qn = len(qidx)
    chunk = 64

    for s in range(0, len(deltas), chunk):
        d = deltas[s:s + chunk]
        dcol = d[:, None]
        vals = F + A * dcol

        if qn:
            for idx in qidx:
                vals[:, idx] = neg_inf
            mx = np.max(vals, axis=1)
        else:
            mx = np.max(vals, axis=1)

        out[s:s + chunk] = mx
        if mu != 0.0:
            out[s:s + chunk] += mu * d

        for t in range(qn):
            idx = qidx[t]
            vq = F[idx] + A[idx] * d + qcoef[t] * d * d
            out[s:s + chunk] = np.maximum(out[s:s + chunk], vq)

    return out

def choose_grid_delta(F, A, dmin, dmax, qidx, qcoef, mu, cur_max, grid_points, refine_points=257):
    span = dmax - dmin
    if span <= 1e-12:
        return 0.0, cur_max

    best_delta = 0.0
    best_val = cur_max

    G = int(grid_points)
    if span < 1e-3:
        G = max(7, min(G, 33))
    elif G < 5:
        G = 5

    deltas = np.linspace(dmin, dmax, G)
    vals = grid_max_quadratic(F, A, deltas, qidx, qcoef, mu)
    idx = int(np.argmin(vals))

    if vals[idx] < best_val - 1e-12:
        best_val = float(vals[idx])
        best_delta = float(deltas[idx])

    if abs(best_delta) > 1e-12 and 0 < idx < G - 1:
        step = span / float(G - 1)
        lo = max(dmin, best_delta - 3.0 * step)
        hi = min(dmax, best_delta + 3.0 * step)
        if hi - lo > 1e-10:
            deltas2 = np.linspace(lo, hi, int(refine_points))
            vals2 = grid_max_quadratic(F, A, deltas2, qidx, qcoef, mu)
            idx2 = int(np.argmin(vals2))
            if vals2[idx2] < best_val - 1e-12:
                best_val = float(vals2[idx2])
                best_delta = float(deltas2[idx2])

    if best_delta < dmin:
        best_delta = dmin
    elif best_delta > dmax:
        best_delta = dmax

    return best_delta, best_val

def coordinate_pass_single(h, F, n, mu, order, grid_points, refine_points, start_time, stop_time):
    off = n - 1
    qidx = (off,)
    qcoef = (-1.0,)

    for k in order:
        if time.time() >= stop_time:
            break
        cur_max = float(np.max(F))
        old = float(h[k])
        coeff = get_coeff(h, k, off)
        delta, val = choose_grid_delta(
            F, coeff, -old, 1.0 - old, qidx, qcoef,
            float(mu), cur_max, int(grid_points), int(refine_points)
        )
        if abs(delta) > 1e-10:
            if val <= cur_max + 1e-8:
                new = old + delta
                if new < 0.0:
                    new = 0.0
                elif new > 1.0:
                    new = 1.0
                eff = new - old
                if abs(eff) > 1e-12:
                    h[k] = new
                    F += coeff * eff
                    F[off] -= eff * eff
    return h, F

def local_search_single(h, n, mu, start_time, stop_time, max_passes, adapt_lam, rng,
                        grid_points=None, refine_points=257):
    if grid_points is None:
        if n <= 120:
            grid_points = 73
        elif n <= 220:
            grid_points = 57
        else:
            grid_points = 49

    target = 0.5 * n
    h = project_sum(h, target, use_shift=False)
    F = compute_F(h)

    best_h = h.copy()
    best_val = compute_c5(h)

    lam = float(mu)
    passes = 0
    stall = 0

    while passes < max_passes and time.time() < stop_time:
        if rng is not None:
            if rng.random_sample() < 0.6:
                order = rng.permutation(n)
            else:
                order = np.arange(n)
        else:
            order = range(n)

        coordinate_pass_single(
            h, F, n, lam, order, grid_points, refine_points,
            time.time(), stop_time
        )
        passes += 1

        drift = float(np.sum(h)) - target
        if adapt_lam:
            mu_target = drift
            if mu_target > 3.0:
                mu_target = 3.0
            elif mu_target < -3.0:
                mu_target = -3.0
            lam = 0.7 * lam + 0.3 * mu_target

        if abs(drift) > 0.05 * n:
            h = project_sum(h, target, use_shift=True)
        else:
            h = project_sum(h, target, use_shift=False)

        F = compute_F(h)
        val = c5_from_F(n, F)

        if val < best_val - 1e-12:
            best_val = val
            best_h = h.copy()
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1

        if stall > (40 if adapt_lam else 15):
            break

    best_h = project_sum(best_h, target, use_shift=False)
    return best_h, compute_c5(best_h)

def pair_pass(h, F, n, step, grid_points, refine_points, start_time, stop_time):
    off = n - 1

    for i in range(n):
        if time.time() >= stop_time:
            break
        p = (i + step) % n
        if p == i:
            continue

        old_i = float(h[i])
        old_p = float(h[p])
        pair_sum = old_i + old_p

        lo = pair_sum - 1.0
        if lo < 0.0:
            lo = 0.0
        hi = pair_sum
        if hi > 1.0:
            hi = 1.0

        dmin = lo - old_i
        dmax = hi - old_i
        if dmax <= dmin + 1e-12:
            continue

        coeff_i = get_coeff(h, i, off)
        coeff_p = get_coeff(h, p, off)
        A = coeff_i - coeff_p

        ad = abs(i - p)
        q1 = off + ad
        q2 = off - ad

        if i < p:
            Q1 = -1.0
            Q2 = 1.0
        else:
            Q1 = 1.0
            Q2 = -1.0

        qidx = (off, q1, q2)
        qcoef = (-2.0, Q1, Q2)

        cur_max = float(np.max(F))
        delta, val = choose_grid_delta(
            F, A, dmin, dmax, qidx, qcoef,
            0.0, cur_max, int(grid_points), int(refine_points)
        )

        if abs(delta) > 1e-10 and val <= cur_max + 1e-9:
            new_i = old_i + delta
            if new_i < 0.0:
                new_i = 0.0
            elif new_i > 1.0:
                new_i = 1.0

            new_p = pair_sum - new_i
            if new_p < 0.0 or new_p > 1.0:
                new_p = 1.0 if new_p > 1.0 else 0.0
                new_i = pair_sum - new_p

            eff = new_i - old_i
            if abs(eff) > 1e-12:
                h[i] = new_i
                h[p] = pair_sum - new_i
                F += A * eff
                d2 = eff * eff
                F[off] += -2.0 * d2
                F[q1] += Q1 * d2
                F[q2] += Q2 * d2

    return h, F

def local_search_pair(h, n, start_time, stop_time, max_passes, rng,
                      grid_points=None, refine_points=257):
    if grid_points is None:
        if n <= 120:
            grid_points = 73
        elif n <= 220:
            grid_points = 57
        else:
            grid_points = 49

    target = 0.5 * n
    h = project_sum(h, target, use_shift=False)
    F = compute_F(h)

    best_h = h.copy()
    best_val = compute_c5(h)

    steps = []
    for s in (1, n - 1, 2, n - 2, 3, n - 3, 5, n - 5, 10, n - 10,
              n // 3, max(1, n // 3 - 1), n // 2):
        if 1 <= s < n and s not in steps:
            steps.append(s)
    if not steps:
        steps = [1]

    passes = 0
    stall = 0

    while passes < max_passes and time.time() < stop_time:
        if rng is not None:
            if passes % 5 == 4:
                step = int(rng.randint(1, n))
            else:
                step = steps[passes % len(steps)]
        else:
            step = steps[passes % len(steps)]

        pair_pass(h, F, n, step, grid_points, refine_points, time.time(), stop_time)
        passes += 1

        if passes % 10 == 0:
            h = project_sum(h, target, use_shift=False)
            F = compute_F(h)

        val = c5_from_F(n, F)
        if val < best_val - 1e-12:
            best_val = val
            best_h = h.copy()
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1

        if stall > 50:
            break

    best_h = project_sum(best_h, target, use_shift=False)
    return best_h, compute_c5(best_h)

def resample_h(h_old, n_old, n_new, target, mode=0):
    if n_old == n_new:
        return project_sum(np.array(h_old, dtype=float, copy=True), target, use_shift=True)

    x_old = 2.0 * np.arange(n_old) / float(n_old)
    x_new = 2.0 * np.arange(n_new) / float(n_new)

    if mode == 1:
        cell = 2.0 / float(n_old)
        idx = np.floor(x_new / cell).astype(int) % n_old
        h_new = h_old[idx]
    else:
        h_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, h_old, left=h_old[0], right=h_old[-1])

    return project_sum(h_new, target, use_shift=True)

def perturb_h(h, scale, rng):
    h2 = h + rng.randn(len(h)) * float(scale)
    return project_sum(h2, 0.5 * len(h), use_shift=True)

def random_binary_h(n, rng):
    u = rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0, n)
    h = (u >= float(np.median(u))).astype(float)
    return project_sum(h, 0.5 * n, use_shift=True)

def thue_morse_h(n):
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=int)
    for i in range(1, n):
        a[i] = a[i >> 1] ^ (i & 1)
    h = a.astype(float)
    return project_sum(h, 0.5 * n, use_shift=True)

def make_default_start(n, rng):
    x = 2.0 * np.arange(n) / float(n)
    h = 0.5 + 0.5 * np.cos(np.pi * x) + 0.01 * rng.randn(n)
    return project_sum(h, 0.5 * n, use_shift=True)

def better_solution(cand_h, cand_c5, best_h, best_c5, best_n):
    if cand_c5 < best_c5 - 1e-12:
        return cand_h, cand_c5, len(cand_h)
    return best_h, best_c5, best_n

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    stop_global = t0 + max(1.0, budget - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    try:
        parent = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        parent = make_default_start(96, rng)

    if parent.size == 0:
        parent = make_default_start(96, rng)

    parent = project_sum(parent, 0.5 * parent.size, use_shift=False)
    best_h = parent.copy()
    best_n = len(best_h)
    best_c5 = compute_c5(best_h)

    # Phase 1: constrained pair-coordinate descent on the parent resolution.
    phase1_stop = t0 + 0.28 * budget
    if phase1_stop < t0 + 10.0:
        phase1_stop = min(stop_global, t0 + 10.0)
    if phase1_stop > stop_global:
        phase1_stop = stop_global

    if time.time() < phase1_stop:
        h2, c2 = local_search_pair(best_h, best_n, t0, phase1_stop, 1000000, rng)
        best_h, best_c5, best_n = better_solution(h2, c2, best_h, best_c5, best_n)

    # Phase 2: single-coordinate descent with adaptive sum penalty.
    phase2_stop = t0 + 0.52 * budget
    if phase2_stop < t0 + 10.0:
        phase2_stop = min(stop_global, t0 + 10.0)
    if phase2_stop > stop_global:
        phase2_stop = stop_global

    if time.time() < phase2_stop:
        h2, c2 = local_search_single(best_h, best_n, 0.0, t0, phase2_stop, 1000000, True, rng)
        best_h, best_c5, best_n = better_solution(h2, c2, best_h, best_c5, best_n)

    # Phase 3: fixed-penalty single-coordinate polishing.
    phase3_stop = t0 + 0.64 * budget
    if phase3_stop > stop_global:
        phase3_stop = stop_global
    mus = (0.0, 0.05, -0.05, 0.15, -0.15, 0.35, -0.35)

    for idx_mu, mu in enumerate(mus):
        if time.time() >= phase3_stop:
            break
        n_left = max(1, len(mus) - idx_mu)
        now = time.time()
        slice_stop = min(phase3_stop, now + max(5.0, (phase3_stop - now) / float(n_left)))
        if slice_stop <= now + 1e-3:
            break
        h2, c2 = local_search_single(best_h, best_n, mu, t0, slice_stop, 200000, False, rng)
        best_h, best_c5, best_n = better_solution(h2, c2, best_h, best_c5, best_n)

    # Phase 4: perturbations and alternate deterministic starts at the current resolution.
    phase4_stop = t0 + 0.76 * budget
    if phase4_stop > stop_global:
        phase4_stop = stop_global

    starts = []
    for sc in (0.01, 0.02, 0.05):
        starts.append(perturb_h(best_h, sc, rng))
    starts.append(random_binary_h(best_n, rng))
    starts.append(thue_morse_h(best_n))

    for idx_st, st in enumerate(starts):
        if time.time() >= phase4_stop:
            break
        n_left = max(1, len(starts) - idx_st)
        now = time.time()
        slice_stop = min(phase4_stop, now + max(5.0, (phase4_stop - now) / float(n_left)))
        if slice_stop <= now + 1e-3:
            break
        h2, c2 = local_search_single(st, len(st), 0.0, t0, slice_stop, 100000, True, rng)
        best_h, best_c5, best_n = better_solution(h2, c2, best_h, best_c5, best_n)

    # Phase 5: resolution transfer / cross-resolution searches.
    phase5_stop = t0 + 0.90 * budget
    if phase5_stop > stop_global:
        phase5_stop = stop_global

    candidates = []
    base_n = best_n if best_n % 2 == 0 else best_n + 1
    for nc in (base_n, 96, 100, 120, 150, 190, 240):
        if 20 <= nc <= 500 and nc not in candidates:
            candidates.append(nc)

    for idx_n, n_new in enumerate(candidates):
        if time.time() >= phase5_stop:
            break
        if n_new == best_n:
            continue

        n_left = max(1, len(candidates) - idx_n)
        now = time.time()
        slice_stop = min(phase5_stop, now + max(5.0, (phase5_stop - now) / float(n_left)))
        if slice_stop <= now + 1e-3:
            break

        binary_like = float(np.mean((np.abs(best_h) < 0.01) | (np.abs(best_h - 1.0) < 0.01)))
        mode = 1 if binary_like > 0.75 else 0

        h_res = resample_h(best_h, best_n, n_new, 0.5 * n_new, mode=mode)
        h2, c2 = local_search_single(h_res, n_new, 0.0, t0, slice_stop, 100000, True, rng)
        best_h, best_c5, best_n = better_solution(h2, c2, best_h, best_c5, best_n)

        if time.time() < slice_stop:
            h3, c3 = local_search_pair(best_h, best_n, t0, slice_stop, 100000, rng)
            best_h, best_c5, best_n = better_solution(h3, c3, best_h, best_c5, best_n)

    # Final polish.
    if time.time() < stop_global:
        h2, c2 = local_search_single(best_h, best_n, 0.0, t0, stop_global, 1000000, True, rng)
        best_h, best_c5, best_n = better_solution(h2, c2, best_h, best_c5, best_n)

    # Final exact feasibility.
    target = 0.5 * best_n
    best_h = project_sum(best_h, target, use_shift=False)
    final_c5 = compute_c5(best_h)

    return best_h, final_c5, best_n
```
